->The Beginning, the Christ, and the End<- <note>original Greek: "a. X. ω." (Alpha, Chi, Omega)</note>

## To the Kind Reader

What I share here, I can certainly assure you, is that these are purely true *Experiments* <term>Experimente: practical laboratory trials or tests</term> which actually deliver through *practical handiwork* <note>Hand-Arbeit refers to the physical labor performed at the alchemical furnace</note> exactly what they promise. What the title shows you, you will indeed see in this small, yet invaluable little work. The Artists <note>Artisten: a term for practitioners of the alchemical art</note> will wonder that I promise mostly *particular processes* <term>Particularia: alchemical procedures aimed at specific transmutations rather than the "Universal" Philosopher's Stone</term>, apart from the so-called *Universal Source* <note>original: "Fonte Universali"</note>, and that I do not follow the opinions of other *Adepts* <note>Adeptorum: masters who have supposedly achieved the Great Work</note>. I have already sufficiently addressed the grounds and reasons why I am able to do this in my recently published *Theoretical-Practical Theo-Philosophy* <note>Theo-Philosophia Theoretico-Practica</note>, to which I now wish to refer the valued reader. In this present little work, I likewise proceed from common things, according to the foundation...

<summary>In this preface, the author introduces a collection of alchemical experiments, emphasizing their practical reliability and focusing on "particular" transmutations rather than traditional universal theories.</summary>
<keywords>alchemical experiments, manual labor, laboratory practice, Particularia, Universal Source, Theo-Philosophy, alchemical adepts</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the preface from the previous leaf. The author is explaining his choice of terminology, transitioning from his specific "Theo-Philosophical" system to standard alchemical language for the sake of the reader's clarity.</meta>

and foundation of the great equality of Nature / according to its seven forms <note>7. Gestalten: This likely refers to the "Seven Properties of Nature" popularized by the mystic Jacob Böhme, a framework used to describe the transition from the spiritual to the material world.</note> and the two principles revealing themselves therein, Fire and Light / which doctrine I have extensively explained in my aforementioned treatise:

But because it requires a deep foundation / and a mind enlightened by God / I do not know / whether everyone will also understand <note>original: "capiren," from the Latin *capere*, meaning to grasp or comprehend.</note> me. For which reason I also wish for now, in these works, to accommodate myself to the terms of the ordinary chemists / and direct myself according to their three principles, <term>Salt 🜔</term>, <term>Sulfur 🜍</term>, and <term>Mercury ☿</term> <note>These three symbols represent the *Tria Prima*, the three primary substances believed by Paracelsian alchemists to compose all matter.</note>, and thus conduct my manner of speech: 

Because I otherwise share this little work for the benefit of each and every poor person / so they may draw an advantage and nourishment from this Golden Source: so that they might not be confused in their *practice* <term>Praxi: the actual physical laboratory work</term> by new terminology / but might all the better achieve their purpose. Otherwise / to understand the ground and foundation of these processes / it would have been very easy for me / to speak according to my own principles / and to adorn the shared processes with a philosophical discourse; 

But it is not my purpose / to philosophize much / which only gives birth to bickering / especially if one does not wish to, nor can, adapt and accommodate oneself to the accustomed manner (which, however, is often all too insufficient to demonstrate a matter from its foundation); but rather I look toward [presenting] truths to the world—

<summary>The author explains that he will use common alchemical terms (Salt, Sulfur, Mercury) rather than his own complex "Seven Forms" of nature to ensure that even "poor persons" can understand and profit from his laboratory processes.</summary>

to present / which require no philosophizing / but rather the truth of my philosophy, as set forth for reflection in the aforementioned treatise / may the fire <note>In alchemy, the "fire" of the furnace was considered the ultimate judge of a theory's truth; if the chemical reaction worked, the philosophy was "legitimized."</note> make known and legitimize in the [laboratory] work. Great words are plentiful enough in the world / but few truths. When the matters / which I here release into public print / have first been experimented upon by a devotee <note>original: "Liebhaber" — literally "lover," referring here to a "lover of the art" or a dedicated practitioner of alchemy.</note> / then people will marvel / how I came to the resolution / to lay such secret matters so openly, with all manual operations, before the eyes of the whole world / and only then search after the depth of my philosophy. One must only see to it / (as I must always remind you) that one brings a sincere heart / with a true intent, to such works / and indeed, above all else, strives for the "one thing needful" <note>A biblical allusion (Luke 10:42) frequently used by Rosicrucians to emphasize spiritual focus over material greed.</note> / so that one does not fall into a confused labyrinth of worldly vanities. There is one more thing I wished to touch upon on this occasion:

Recently, through my first treatise, titled: *True and Perfect Preparation of the Philosopher’s Stone of the Brotherhood from the Order of the Golden and Rosy Cross* <note>original: "Wahrhaffte und vollkomene Bereitung des Philosophischen Steins der Brüderschafft aus dem Orden des Goldnen und Rosen-Creuzes"</note> etc., a possessor of the Hermetic sciences was awakened and prompted / to publish a treatise / titled: *Antrum naturae & Artis reclusum* <note>original Latin: "The Recessed Cave of Nature and Art Disclosed"</note>, or Secret-fill-

He / dearest friend / longs to enter into the community and acquaintance of the said **Brotherhood** / and to be received by them; which / if he loves **GOD** above all /

## Chapter I.

### Contains the matters / which are treated in the present treatise <term>Tractat: a formal written work dealing systematically with a specific subject.</term>.

I have promised the kind reader and worthy seeker of nature's secrets <term>Natur Geheimnisse: the hidden spiritual and physical laws of the universe, often associated with alchemy and Hermeticism.</term> a source of gold <term>Gold-Quelle: an alchemical metaphor for both the literal creation of gold and the discovery of spiritual truth.</term> on the title page; and indeed, I do not intend to promise great things behind which nothing but darkness and perhaps even untruth are hidden—as is commonly done—but I intend to speak according to the truth. Thus, what the title promises shall also be found to be so in reality <note>original: "in Re" — in the thing itself, or in practice.</note>, so that one may safely follow the work and expect a desired outcome, provided one has also entered into a true friendship with **GOD**. I write from a sincere spirit and heartfelt love for my neighbor; how it will be received by him, I commend to **GOD**.

The kind reader will have seen in my recent Theo-Philosophy <note>Theo-Philosophia: a term used by writers like Paracelsus to describe a philosophy grounded in divine revelation and the study of nature.</note> for what reason I [depart] from the common manner of the others...

<sig>A</sig>
<meta>catchword: Ade</meta>

<summary>The author begins the first chapter by promising a truthful guide to nature's secrets and a 'source of gold,' emphasizing that success in these alchemical pursuits requires a sincere heart and a right relationship with God.</summary>
<keywords>secrets of nature, source of gold, Theo-Philosophy, treatise, truth, sincerity, alchemical practice</keywords>

--- Page 14 ---
<meta>This page continues the introduction to the author’s practical alchemical method, transitioning from the theoretical "Theo-Philosophy" mentioned on the previous page to the specific "handiwork" of laboratory experiments. The author distinguishes his approachable methods from the long, grueling process of the "Great Work."</meta>

...depart from the way of the Adepts <term>Adeptorum: the Latin plural for Adepts, individuals who have supposedly mastered the secrets of alchemy.</term> and recommend other truths beyond the so-called Universal Source <note>original: "Fonte Universali" — the primary, singular root or "spring" of all natural things in Hermetic philosophy.</note>. The reason is that my philosophy is different from what has been commonly held, and it has a far deeper foundation. From this foundation is revealed the great equality in nature, wherein one thing willingly accepts another and thereby allows itself to be improved, exalted, and transformed—as can be seen in my aforementioned Theo-Philosophy.

For even now, I communicate only a few true experiments from my own handiwork <note>original: "Hand-Arbeit" — practical, manual laboratory labor as opposed to purely theoretical study.</note> using common metals. The work involved is pleasant and easy, and it is not as long and tedious as that which comes from the Universal Matter <term>Universal-Materie: the "First Matter" or Prima Materia, the legendary starting material for the Philosopher's Stone.</term> (excepting the Great Work <note>original: "Opus maximum" — the ultimate alchemical process of creating the Philosopher's Stone.</note>), which requires much time, great diligence, effort, caution, and patience, though it does not require great expense. Certain and true works will be communicated here, the foundation of which is always based on a certain and solid experiment; from these, ever higher and greater works subsequently flow.

The materials from which they are prepared are all common metals and minerals that can be had and obtained in all places. The main subjects from which certain benefit can be gained are <term>Gold</term> <note>symbol: ☉</note>, <term>Silver</term> <note>symbol: ☽</note>, and <term>Mercury</term> <note>symbol: ☿</note>, which other metals and minerals assist by imparting their power to them. This will be seen in the following experiments, where first some works concerning <term>Silver</term> <note>symbol: ☽</note> shall be shown, so that also

<summary>The author explains that his alchemical approach differs from tradition by focusing on accessible, "pleasant" experiments with common metals rather than the tedious pursuit of the Universal Matter. He identifies gold, silver, and mercury as the primary materials for the practical works that follow.</summary>

Those who, because of the costliness of **Gold** <term>☉: the astrological and alchemical symbol for gold, the "solar" metal.</term>, might have to hold back, may nonetheless work with a cheaper material and thereby make provision for the other. Afterwards, several perfect pieces and works made from **Gold** <term>☉</term> shall also be shown, along with some other experiments from other materials, all of which will have their complete accuracy, so that they may also be followed safely and without fear. I say once more: these are certain truths that I communicate, which anyone may safely follow and certainly expect the promised benefit. Finally, I will also add some true matters from the Ancients who were likewise occupied with such manipulations and works; these will provide their processes, a proper foundation, and their correct benefit and profit to a clever and sensible worker who understands the fire and is practiced in all **chemical** <term>Chymischen: referring to "Chymistry," the early modern discipline that combined experimental chemistry with alchemical theory.</term> manipulations. I can assure the worthy reader of this from my own **experience** <note>original: "Experienz" — practical, firsthand knowledge gained through laboratory work rather than book learning.</note> and manual labor; otherwise, I would not recommend them but would rather have left them out. However, there are subtle manipulations involved that require an agile mind and an experienced worker. And because some might receive this book who are still inexperienced in **chemistry** <term>Chymie</term> and its ordinary manipulations, but who would nonetheless like to derive some benefit from it—which they, since they do not know the common manipulations...

...[to practice them], or be forced to refrain [from them], and since the appropriate books in which they are described might not be known to them or immediately at hand; therefore, I have deemed it good to include them as an **appendix** <term>Anhang</term> at the conclusion, so that one may immediately examine them and learn the practical **manipulations** <term>Handgriffe: literally "hand-grips," referring to the physical techniques and manual skills required in a laboratory.</term> occurring in those **processes** <term>Processen</term>. Finally, I wish—as always, so also now—to give a well-meaning reminder: that one must first have died to the world and "put on" **Christ** <note>A biblical reference to Galatians 3:27; "putting on Christ" implies a spiritual transformation where the believer's identity is replaced by the divine.</note> in true denial of one's earthly life according to flesh and blood, and be essentially reunited with Him as the true **source** <term>Quelle</term> of all things. Otherwise, the **blessing** <term>Seegen</term> will depart, and the goal will be difficult to attain, even if one already holds the complete **recipe** <term>Recept</term> in hand; for **GOD** holds the rudder, and above all things, one must be in good standing with Him.

For I already have experience through letters that some succeeded with the "certain **particular**" <term>Particular: in alchemy, a "particular" process is one that transforms a specific amount of base metal into a precious one, as opposed to the "Universal" work of the Philosopher's Stone.</term> I communicated in the first **treatise** <term>Tractat</term> I published, and were thereby rescued from their poverty. Conversely, for others it did not work; for still others it worked, but was then brought to naught through ignorant oversight. In this, I have seen **God's** hand most wonderfully, and from the letters, I have quite clearly recognized the disposition of each person, finding that their purpose was either not pure at all, or at least wavering, and that their hearts clung more to earthly things than to **GOD**.

For which reason I

...I am also now writing such secret truths freely and openly to the world; although I was of a nearly different mind during the elaboration of my **Theo-Philosophia Theoretico-Practica** <term>Theo-Philosophia Theoretico-Practica: A "Theoretical-Practical God-Philosophy," a term the author uses to describe a system merging theology with the practical study of nature (alchemy).</term>; so that only a few **experiments** <term>Experimente: In this context, these are specific alchemical trials or laboratory procedures.</term>—all of which nevertheless possess their truth—were communicated there / and now and then a small **technique** <term>Handgriff: Literally "hand-grip," referring to a manual procedure, practical trick, or a specific stage of a laboratory process.</term> was omitted, leaving it for further reflection. Indeed, to the wise and the pious, it was clear enough. 

But now, as I become aware of God’s provident governance and foresight over it, let it be; the kind reader can easily imagine that the **resolution** <term>Resolution: A firm decision or determination.</term> for the **publication** <term>Publication</term> of such secret matters and true experiments was difficult for me to reach / when on one hand I looked upon the evil world / which misuses all of God’s good gifts for its own vanity; but when, on the other hand, I considered my poor, suffering neighbor / and in the midst of it all, the most wise GOD / who holds the **directorate** <term>Directorium: The supreme guidance, management, or "steering" of all affairs.</term> over everything / and does not allow everyone to have all that they desire / but often wonderfully permits an obstacle to thwart this or that person’s certain and intended schemes / then I finally dared it: Especially when I consider my calling from GOD / as I find myself now drawn by GOD with all power through His Spirit to look upon the invisible GOD alone / and to stand before the **throng** <note>original: "Presse." While "Presse" can mean a printing press, in this spiritual context it likely refers to a "throng" or "crowd" of people, or perhaps the "pressure/distress" of his brothers.</note> of my brothers as a spiritual **Melchizedekian priest** <note>A reference to the biblical figure Melchizedek (Genesis 14), who was both king and priest. In mystical traditions, the "Order of Melchizedek" represents a spiritual priesthood that exists outside of formal church institutions, focused on direct divine inspiration.</note>,...

<summary>The author explains his decision to openly publish secret alchemical experiments and truths, overcoming his initial hesitation caused by the fear that the wicked might misuse this knowledge. He attributes this change of heart to his sense of duty toward his suffering neighbors and his divine calling as a spiritual priest.</summary>

<keywords>Theo-Philosophia, secrecy, experiments, divine providence, Melchizedek, spiritual priesthood, publication, vanity</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the author’s spiritual and philosophical justification for his work. ...I find, therefore, that for the sake of the **noble little pearl** <note>original: "Perlein," a common alchemical and mystical metaphor for the "Pearl of Great Price" or the hidden spark of divine wisdom within the soul.</note>, I regard all my experience and knowledge as merely filth / and nothing can satisfy or calm me except my eternal heavenly Virgin **Sophia** <term>Sophia: The personification of Divine Wisdom, often viewed in Christian mysticism as a bridge between the human soul and the Divine.</term>, revealed in **Christ JESUS**; likewise, I have also found it right to share my **gift** <note>Refers to his alchemical knowledge and laboratory skills.</note>, which I possess, with others who may possess and employ it better than I. I have exchanged the earthly **tincture** <term>Tincture: In alchemy, a substance used to "color" or transform metals; here used metaphorically to mean a spiritual essence or state of being.</term> for the heavenly one / and I have not yet regretted my trade / especially since I found myself most inwardly drawn and called to it; let another use earthly treasures / and make friends for himself with unrighteous **Mammon** <term>Mammon: A biblical term for material wealth or greed, often personified as a false god.</term> / so that when he is in need / he may be received by them into the eternal dwellings. <note>A reference to the Parable of the Unjust Steward in the Gospel of Luke, chapter 16.</note>

I turn now to the matter itself / and make a beginning with **silver**.

### Chapter II.

### An experiment by which Venus <note>original: ♀ (the symbol for Venus or copper)</note> is brought into Luna <note>original: ☽ (the symbol for Luna or silver)</note> and Sol <note>original: ☉ (the symbol for Sol or gold)</note> / as a foundation for other works.

In this present chapter, we wish to establish an **experiment** <term>Experiment: In early modern science, this refers to a specific, repeatable laboratory procedure.</term> as a foundation / from which many higher works shall flow hereafter. It is, however, such a certain experiment that no one can overturn it / and it always succeeds; whoever tries it will find the truth. It is as follows:

### I. Fixation of the Moon <term>Moon: The alchemical name and symbol (☽) for Silver.</term>.

I have indeed already noted various ways in which the Moon [Silver] is commonly purified and its Mercury <term>Mercury: Represented by the symbol ☿; here referring to the "spirit" or metallic essence extracted from silver.</term> is digested in my recently published **Theoretical-Practical Theo-Philosophy**; however, I wish now to disclose the true foundation by which its fixation <term>Fixation: The chemical or alchemical process of making a volatile or "fleeing" substance stable under heat, so it does not evaporate or change.</term> is achieved, and in which substance it primarily resides. We must never set aside the general rule of the great Hermes <note>Refers to Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary father of the Hermetic tradition and alchemy.</note>, but always contemplate it: *That which is below is as that which is above, and that which is above is as that which is below* <note>original Latin: "Quod inferius est sicut superius, & superius sicut inferius." This is the core tenet of the Emerald Tablet, suggesting a fundamental correspondence between the earthly realm (metals) and the divine or celestial realm.</note>, so that the lower is entirely equal to the upper, and the upper in all things compares to the lower.

Take 2 parts calamine, 4 parts common salt <term>Common Salt: Latin: *sal commune*; used here in a purification process.</term>, and mix them. Place this into a cementation crucible, with the prepared silver <term>Silver: Represented by the symbol for the Moon (☽).</term> in the middle, and more of the powder on top. Let it flow for 14 hours while sealed with clay. Then, take it out and proceed further as follows, after the silver has been previously cupelled <term>Cupellation: A refining process where metals are heated in a shallow porous cup (a cupel) to separate precious metals like gold and silver from base metals like lead.</term>.

Now you must have the following cementation powder: 1 part antimony <term>Antimony: Represented by the symbol ♁; known in alchemy for its ability to purify gold and silver by "devouring" base metals.</term> [and] quicklime, 1/2 part common good table salt, and 1/4 part raw tartar <note>Tartar: A potassium salt (cream of tartar) found in wine barrels; used as a flux to help metals melt and purify.</note>—it matters not whether it is white or red. Everything should be well crushed and mixed together; thus, the cementation powder for the prepared silver is ready. One is not strictly bound to this specific recipe; whoever knows a better one may use it. If one uses sea salt instead of common salt, it is even better. Above all, antimony is the most perfect master for robbing the silver of its Venusian soul <note>Venusian soul: A reference to copper impurities. Since the planet Venus corresponds to the metal Copper, the author views copper contamination as a "soul" or quality that must be stripped away to leave pure silver.</note> and for drying it out, which is important to note. Now take your silver prepared with lead <term>Lead: Represented by the symbol for Saturn (♄).</term> and beat it into thin sheets, barely the thickness of the back of a knife. With these and the mentioned powder, create a layer-upon-layer <term>Layer-upon-layer: Latin: *Stratum super stratum* (S.S.S.); a method of packing a crucible with alternating layers of metal and chemical reagents.</term> arrangement in a cementation box, such that the powder first lies a finger thick at the bottom, and then again just as thick on top. It is arranged so that the box becomes full. Afterwards, seal another crucible or a lid on top with good lute <term>Lute: A fire-resistant clay or cement used to seal alchemical vessels airtight.</term>, and let everything dry well. Then, place it in a cementation fire between four bricks with coals; the coals must all be "dead" <note>Dead coals: This likely refers to coals that have been previously burned and are now glowing steadily without active flames, providing a gentle, consistent heat.</note>...

Stir and let it boil / after a good hour, pour it off again to join the previous liquid / and keep adding fresh urine to the **litharge** <note>original: "Silberglätte"; lead monoxide, often a byproduct of silver refining.</note> / as long as there is any left. In this way, one can prepare a large supply of urine / which becomes quite brown from the **litharge** and as thick as beer. The fire can be maintained with good coals / and do not worry about the time / but always let the urine boil down slowly to half its volume / because in this manner, the urine draws the **litharge** into itself much more strongly. The reason for this is that after its **phlegm** <note>In alchemy, "phlegm" refers to the watery, inactive portion of a liquid that evaporates first.</note> has boiled away / the urine becomes sharp and concentrated / and only then truly begins to attack the **litharge**. This is now a precious urine [saturated] with the **litharge**.

Now take 12 **lot** <note>A "lot" (Loth) is a historical German unit of weight, roughly equivalent to 14–15 grams or half an ounce.</note> of <term>Antimony</term> <note>original: "♁" / "Spießglas"; antimony trisulfide, a metallic mineral used to purify gold.</note> in its crude state / 3 **lot** of common **cinnabar** <note>Mercury sulfide, prized for its deep red color.</note> / and 2 **lot** of common **verdigris** <note>A green pigment or crust that forms on copper; used here as a chemical reagent.</note>. These items are to be ground very small on a stone for one hour / then placed into a shortened **flask** / which should be quite wide at the neck / and also somewhat tall / so that nothing overflows. Pour about one good **noessel** <note>original: "Nössel"; a liquid measure roughly equal to half a liter.</note> of the urine prepared with the **litharge**—making sure it is quite clear and free of sediment—onto it. Place the **flask** in a **sand bath** <note>original: "Sand-Capelle"; a container filled with sand that is heated to provide a steady, even temperature to the glass flask.</note> / and apply fire / so that it boils. Stir it diligently / and take care / that it does not overflow. Boil it in this manner for 8 hours straight / in one continuous session / in such a way that you are always adding more of the prepared urine / and stirring frequently with a wooden spatula / until more than 2 **cans** of such urine have been added / even if it already [more...]

When the cementation is finished / take the silver out / scrape the cementing powder clean from it / and melt it. This cementing process can be repeated 4 times / and then the silver may be used.

## II. Extraction of <term>Antimony</term> <note>original: "♁"; the symbol for antimony, here likely referring to a specific metallic essence or "soul" extracted from copper.</note> from <term>Copper</term> <note>original: "♀ere"; from the Latin 'venere,' referring to Venus, the planetary name for copper.</note>.

This extraction can occur in various ways; yet one is always better and more excellent than the others. And because we have already shown one method in our first treatise, titled *True and Perfect Preparation of the Philosophical Stone*, etc., in the **Particular** <note>A "Particular" refers to a specific chemical recipe or process, often for the transmutation of base metals, as opposed to the "Universal" Great Work.</note> attached at the end—which is one of the best and shortest—we wish to retain the same here as well. And because perhaps not everyone might possess my first treatise, I wish to repeat it once more, and also add 2 other methods for obtaining such <term>Antimony</term> <note>original: "♁".</note>, from which one may afterwards choose what they wish.

### I. Method
### For extracting the <term>Antimony</term> <note>original: "♁".</note> from <term>Copper</term> <note>original: "♀nere".</note>.

Take 1 part of pure copper; dissolve it in 8 parts of unprecipitated <term>Aqua Fortis</term> <note>original: "Aq. Fort."; Latin for "strong water," referring to nitric acid.</note> so that everything is dissolved; evaporate or distill half of the *Aqua Fortis* away through an **alembic** <note>original: "Alembicum"; a helmet-like apparatus used in distillation.</note> in a suitable flask. Before the flask becomes cold—to prevent it from crystallizing, which happens very easily—add a half-part of a good **Oil of Vitriol** <note>original: "Oleum Vitrioli"; concentrated sulfuric acid.</note> while it is still warm. For example: if there was 1 **pound** <note>original: "℔".</note> of copper <note>original: "♀".</note>, add ½ pound of the oil. Swirl it well together; let it stand for a few days if you wish, but if not, you may also take it immediately, and <term>abstract</term> <note>To "abstract" in this context means to remove a liquid by distillation or evaporation.</note> the *Aqua Fortis* from it until it is nearly dry—

One must maintain the strong sublimation-fire for 7 hours, making it very strong at the end. ## Chapter VI.
### Experiment.

->How Silver <term>☽</term> is brought by the Sulfur <term>🜍</term> of Venus <term>♀</term> <note>original: "♀neris"; referring to the sulfur extracted from copper</note> to the point where it takes several parts of the silver into itself as permanent Gold <term>☉</term>.<-

---

In this work, two things are mainly required: First, that the Silver <term>☽</term> be made truly fluid like wax and turned into an oil; second, that it be united with the spiritual Sulfur <term>🜍</term> and made rich in "blood" <note>the "blood" of the metals refers to the deep red tincture or essence that signifies the transition to gold</note> with the same.

First, regarding the first preparation, where the Silver <term>☽</term> is made fluid and turned into an oil, it is to be noted that it must be a "fixed silver" <term>☽ fixa</term> <note>original: "☽ fixa"; silver that has been treated to resist acids and heat like gold does</note> which is used for this. This silver must no longer be attacked by any Aqua Fortis <note>original: "Aq. fort."; nitric acid, used to test if a metal is silver or gold</note>, and in all things—in sound and weight—it must be equal to Gold <term>☉</term>, and pass all tests for Gold <term>☉</term> except for the color. This is obtained according to the above description in the following order.

Initially, a fine cupel-silver <term>Capellen-☽</term> <note>silver purified through cupellation, a refining process where ores are heated in a shallow pit or "cupel" to separate precious metals from base metals like lead</note> must be taken, which, as mentioned above, is calcined <note>heated to a high temperature to create a powder</note> and brought to a lime. Afterwards, it is three times imbibed and led out <term>deducirt</term> with Lead <term>♄</term> that has been dissolved by sal ammoniac <term>🜹</term> <note>ammonium chloride</note>. Then, it is cemented with the described Salt-Cement <term>🜔-Cement</term> until it reaches brittleness, exactly as was taught above. This cementation is to be repeated often, so that when it

Once it is accomplished, and the Silver <term>☽</term> is laminated <note>beaten or rolled into thin sheets to increase surface area</note> again and cemented anew, one should not be discouraged, even if one must spend four weeks on it. Since it can be made in large quantities to keep in stock, it will certainly be worth the effort; for through this, the metal is brought to complete fixity <note>the ability to withstand intense heat or acid without evaporating or changing</note>. 

Afterward, it is cemented with the urinous cement <note>original: "Urinosischen Cement"; a mixture likely containing ammonium salts or sal ammoniac derived from urine</note> up to seven times, each time for 8 hours, with careful management of the fire so that the metal does not melt <note>original: "fliesse"; literally "flow"</note>, as was mentioned above. Thus, one will experience such a transformation of the former Silver <term>☽</term> as one would not have believed possible. With this Silver <term>☽</term> thus prepared—which has now become a true "fixed Moon" <note>original: "Luna fixa"</note> and in all things equal to Gold <term>☉</term>—one proceeds as follows:

Take 8 Loth <note>a traditional Germanic unit of weight; 8 Loth is approximately 4 ounces or 115 grams</note> of this fixed Silver <term>☽</term> and dissolve it in a good and pure Aqua Regia <note>a highly corrosive mixture of acids</note>, using as much as is necessary for the solution so that everything is fully dissolved and nothing remains, as the weight must be kept accurate. Separate it from the remaining dregs <note>original: "fæcibus"; the insoluble solid residues</note> through a filter. 

In another glass, dissolve 24 Loth of Mercury <term>☿</term> likewise in Aqua Regia. These two solutions are poured together, placed in a suitable flask, and set in a sand-bath <note>original: "Sand-Capelle"; a container of sand heated from below to provide a steady, gentle heat</note>. The Aqua Regia is gently distilled off <note>original: "abgezogen"; the process of evaporating the liquid and collecting it elsewhere</note> until the material is dry, but in such a way that none of the Mercury <term>☿</term> sublimes <note>turns into vapor and re-solidifies</note>. The distilled Aqua Regia is then poured back onto the residue and distilled off again until dry, once more without allowing the Mercury <term>☿</term> to sublime.

->**The first method / to bring the silver to a point / where it yields its salt.**<-

Take one part of fine **cupelled silver** <term>cupelled silver</term> <note>Silver refined in a cupel to remove impurities like lead.</note> / and one part of common **cinnabar** <term>cinnabar</term> <note>Mercury sulfide; often used in these processes to introduce a "mercurial" quality.</note> broken into long pieces the thickness of a straw / or slightly thicker / place it into a **retort** <term>retort</term> or a **flask** with its **head** <note>original: *Helm*; the cap of the distillation vessel.</note> / place it deep in a **sand bath** / apply **fire** <note>The symbol $\triangle$ represents *Ignis* or fire.</note> from above / then from below / until the **mercury** <note>represented here by a spirit/mercury symbol.</note> has come over / then once removed / the **silver** will be transformed into a **dead lime** <term>dead lime</term> <note>A "calx" or powder; the metal has lost its metallic form and shine.</note> / while the pieces of cinnabar will still lie whole and blackish in the glass. ->**Another method.**<-

**Dissolve** <note>original: *Solvire*.</note> four **lots** <note>A "Lot" is a historical German unit of weight, roughly 16 grams or half an ounce.</note> of fine cupelled silver in a **distilled nitric acid** <term>Aqua fortis</term> <note>Literally "strong water," a powerful solvent for silver.</note> / draw off the **nitric acid** until only half remains / and while it is still warm / **precipitate** <note>original: *præcipitire*; causing a solid to settle out of a solution.</note> this **silver** with **aqua regia** <term>Aqua Regia</term> <note>Literally "Royal Water," a mixture of nitric and hydrochloric acids.</note> or even just common **salt water** / until no more **silver** falls to the bottom as a white **lime**. You would do even better / if instead of **aqua regia** or **salt water**...

...take a good **Oil of Vitriol** <term>Oil of Vitriol</term> <note>original: *Oleum Vitrioli*; concentrated sulfuric acid.</note> / as much as is necessary for **precipitation** <term>precipitation</term>; Take 3 parts of the fixed Tincture / and mix it with 2 parts of the **Oil of the Sulfur of Silver** / let it pass through the colors once again / and repeat this as often as you wish / finally [place it] in a **steam bath** <term>steam bath</term> <note>original: *Balneo vaporoso*.</note> or **horse dung** <term>horse dung</term> <note>original: *fimo Equino*; used as a natural, steady source of low heat for long-term "digestion" or fermentation of the substance.</note>.

### Chapter X.
## Of Gold.

If one desires any benefit from **Gold**, one must see to it that its **tincture** <term>Tincture</term> <note>the concentrated "soul" or color of a metal that has the power to transform other metals.</note> and color are increased; for then it tinges and takes a good portion of **Silver** <note>original: symbol for *Luna*</note> with it to become true **Gold** <note>original: symbol for *Sol*</note>. **For the Sun does not tinge unless it is first tinged** <note>original Latin: *Sol enim non tingit niſi prius tingatur*. One can then very easily arrive at a **tincture** that tinges several parts of fine silver into gold, and likewise can afterwards bring the same through **multiplication** <term>Multiplication</term> <note>the process of increasing the quantity and transmuting power of an alchemical substance.</note> to several thousand parts. I have already revealed the foundation for this in my recently shared **Particular**, and also in the **Theo-Philoso-** <note>The text cuts off here, continuing as "sophia" on the next page.</note>

### I.

### The Manner and Method by which Gold <note>original symbol: $\odot$</note> is Exalted and Heightened in its Color.

In our recently published *Theo-Philosophy* <note>Referring to a previous work by the author, likely focused on the intersection of theology and alchemy.</note>, we set forth various methods by which it is possible to exalt the color of gold; for now, however, we wish to remain with one certain method—namely, the one communicated in the first treatise when this "Particular" first came to light—by means of a **Sulphur of Venus** <term>Sulphur of Venus</term> <note>In alchemy, "Venus" is the name for Copper. The "Sulphur" refers to the internal "soul" or coloring principle of the metal, not common brimstone.</note>. First, it must be shown: 1.) How the Sulfur <note>original symbol: $\triangle$, representing fire or the fiery principle of sulfur.</note> is to be extracted from the copper <note>original symbol: ♀</note> and purified; and 2.) how the fine gold <note>original symbol: $\odot$</note> is to be exalted and heightened with it.

1.) How the Sulfur <note>original symbol: $\triangle$</note> is to be drawn from the copper <note>original symbol: ♀</note> for this work? This has already been mentioned above in the 3rd Chapter, in three different ways. Here, we shall abide by the first way: namely, where one dissolves <term>solviret</term> 1 part of copper in *Aqua fortis* <term>Aqua fortis</term> <note>Nitric acid, used to dissolve metals.</note>. When it is dissolved, distill off <term>abstrahirt</term> half of the liquid. Then, while it is still warm and before it crystallizes, pour in half a part of Oil of Vitriol <note>original: $\text{o}^\text{o}$ Vitrioli; concentrated sulfuric acid.</note> and swirl it well together. Distill off the *Aqua fortis* and repeat this three more times, adding the previously distilled liquid and some fresh *Aqua fortis* each time, so that the Sulfur of the Oil of Vitriol may be thoroughly fixed <term>figire</term> to the Sulphur of Venus. Then, let the remainder be slightly reverberated <term>reverberiret</term> <note>heated in a furnace where the flame is reflected or "reverberated" onto the material.</note> and burned off in molten wax; then its

The **Imbibition** <term>Imbibition</term> <note>The process of "feeding" a solid substance with a liquid so it absorbs it, similar to how a sponge takes up water.</note> occurs thus: Pour onto the **Silver Calx** <term>Calx</term> <note>A powder produced by heating a metal in air; in modern terms, an oxide.</note> as much of the **Water of Saturn** <note>original: ▽ Saturni; likely a solution containing dissolved lead, such as lead acetate or "sugar of lead."</note> until it is like a paste; stir it well together with a small wooden stick; afterwards let the moisture evaporate very gently on the sand <note>A sand bath provided even, indirect heat to glass vessels by nesting them in heated sand.</note> in a glass bowl; when it has dried gently, imbibe it anew and dry it again; repeat this as often as necessary until the one part of the dissolved **Saturn** <note>original symbol: ♄; the alchemical name for lead.</note> has been entirely incorporated into it.

### III.
### How the Fixed Silver is combined and united with the Exalted Gold.

Take your **Fixed Silver** <note>original: Lunæ fixæ</note>, as you have just learned to make it, of the same weight as your **Exalted Gold**. Melt both the **Fixed Silver** and the **Exalted Gold** together. When they have both melted together, hammer it into thin sheets <note>original: laminire; to flatten into plates or leaves to increase surface area.</note> and cement it with the **Urine-based Cement** <note>original: Urinofischen Cement; a purifying mixture likely containing salts derived from urine.</note> that was described to you above in the 3rd chapter. Repeat this 3 or 4 more times, in the same manner as you were taught in the cited chapter. Each time, expose it to heat for 8 hours in a **Circular Fire** <term>Circul-Feuer</term> <note>A furnace designed to provide even heat from all sides.</note>, bringing the temperature to the crucible **by degrees** <note>original: per gradus; slowly and incrementally increasing the heat.</note>. However, ensure that the crucible is not completely touched by the coals, much less becomes red-hot, so that it does not melt and turn into a **Metallic Lump** <term>Regulus</term> <note>A mass of impure metal; here, the author warns that overheating will cause the precious metal to fuse with base lead, ruining the work.</note> with the **Lead** <note>original symbol: ♄</note>; in such a way you would suffer a loss. When this is done, your **Gold** is separated from the cement powder and **confirmed** <note>Stabilized or tested for its final quality.</note> with **Mercury** <term>Mercury</term> <note>original symbol: ☿</note>.

### Strengthening of the Oil with Mercury <note>original: Confirmatio Olis cum ☿ to.</note>

When you have taken your prepared **Gold** <term>Gold</term> <note>original symbol: ☉</note> out of the **Cement** <note>The purifying powder mixture used in previous steps.</note>, granulate it very small, or have it filed into shavings. Mix it with an equal weight of finely rubbed, pure, and unadulterated **Mercury** <term>Mercury</term> <note>original symbol: ☿</note> in a stone mortar. Put it into a melting crucible and let it flow together in a **charcoal fire** <note>original symbol: △, the sign for fire.</note>, and let the mercury smoke off from it, which occurs with very little heat. When all the mercury has departed, take the crucible from the fire, crush the **mass** <note>original: Massam</note>, and rub it into a powder in a stone mortar. Incorporate it into melted wax that has been mixed with some **tallow** <term>Unschlit</term> <note>Animal fat used here to create a paste or "impastation" for the chemical process.</note>; let it cook together for a while, then burn it off.

When this is done, mix the gold powder with **Potash** <term>Potash</term>; put a little crushed **Borax** <term>Borax</term> <note>A flux used to help the metal melt and to dissolve impurities.</note> into the bottom of a melting crucible, and on top of that, place the gold powder mixed with potash. Melt it again, and you will have a perfect **Gold** of a high degree, which remains stable through all **Imperial tests** <term>Reichs-Proben</term> <note>The official standard assays used in the Holy Roman Empire to verify the purity of precious metals.</note>.

Thus, there are 2 parts to observe: the **Gold** <note>original symbol: ☉</note> and then the **Sulphur** <note>original symbol: △; in this context, the active "fire" or dyeing principle.</note> with which it is tinged; finally, the **Mercury** <note>original symbol: ☿</note> which prepares the gold beforehand and is also the medium through which the Gold and Sulphur are centrally united.

We divide the work into 2 operations: the first is the **preparation** <note>original: Præparation</note> of the Gold, where it is made fluid and spiritual through the Mercury; the second, however, is where the Sulphur is prepared and united with the Gold. Each operation has its sub-operations. Here it should be noted that, first, the Gold used must be purified through **Antimony** <note>original symbol: ♄ (Saturn). In alchemy, "purifying gold through Saturn" usually refers to using antimony to devour impurities, as antimony was known as the "Grey Wolf."</note>, and this must be done three times. Afterwards, before being used in the work, it must be **exalted** <note>To "exalt" a metal meant to improve its color, weight, or medicinal properties.</note> by a **Sulphur of Venus** <note>Copper</note> or **Mars** <note>Iron</note>; only then is it prepared with the Mercury.

### I.
### The first operation, in which the Gold is prepared for the work and made ready for conjunction with the Sulphur.

First, for this work, you must not take an inferior **Crown** or **Rhenish gold**, but a good **Hungarian Gold** <note>Hungarian gold was famous in the early modern period for its high natural purity.</note>. You must purify it three times through Antimony; the manner and way of purifying the Gold through Antimony will be described in the practical instructions below. ### Chapter XIII.
### The Regeneration of the Sun,
### or
### How Gold is brought to a Recipe <term>℞</term> that tints all metals into Gold <term>☉</term>, by the separation of its principles and their reunion.

This work is accomplished through the separation of the principles and their recombination; specifically, the gold is separated into its **sulfur** <term>🜍</term> and its **mercurial salt** <term>🜔</term> <note>The symbol 🜔 typically represents salt in this tradition.</note>, or according to my principles, into **fire** and **light**. Now we shall first show how its **sulfur** <term>🜍</term> is to be obtained, and then its **mercurial salt** <term>🜔</term>. How this separation is possible, we have shown above regarding **silver** <term>☽</term>; now we wish only to write the practice and the recipe.

### I.
### Separation of the Sulfur from Gold. <note>original Latin: Separatio Sulfuris ex Sole</note>

First, you must have a good Hungarian gold <note>Hungarian gold was historically prized for its high purity.</note>, cast through **antimony** <term>♁</term>, which, as you were taught, is exalted <note>The process of increasing the "virtue" or intensity of a metal's properties, often its color.</note> with the **sulfur** <term>🜍</term> of **iron** <term>♂</term> or **copper** <term>♀</term>, so that it retains its high color even on the **cupel** <note>A shallow, porous vessel used in assaying to separate precious metals from base metals like lead; original: Capellen.</note> and remains so highly tinted.

Then take at least 4 **loth** <note>A traditional German unit of weight, roughly equivalent to half an ounce or 15 grams.</note> of such exalted gold; prepare it and make it fluid through **mercury sublimate** <note>Mercuric chloride, used as a powerful reagent to "open" the body of the gold.</note>, as taught in the previous process, namely, that you

By repeating this many times, its power increases so much that it can eventually be multiplied a thousandfold.

I say again: these operations are certain and can be safely followed; they will provide what they promise.

## Chapter XIV.

### An excellent Cinnabar experiment, through which one can amply maintain oneself and have his livelihood.

The foundation of this and the following **Cinnabar** <term>Zinober</term> <note>Cinnabar is a bright red mineral consisting of mercury sulfide, the most common ore of mercury.</note> experiments is a certain experiment which was the invention of Baron Schröder <note>Friedrich Wilhelm Freiherr von Schröder (1633–1689) was a German alchemist and economist known for his theories on how alchemy could stabilize a nation's economy.</note>, which we also wish to set down here for the benefit of the reader; and it is as follows:

<margin>Take:</margin> 1 part finely filed **Silver** <note>original: ☽, the symbol for Luna/Silver.</note>, and 1 part common **Cinnabar** broken into longish pieces the thickness of a straw or slightly thicker; put them into a **retort** <term>Retorte</term> <note>A glass vessel with a long, downward-pointing neck used for distilling substances.</note>, place it in a **sand bath** <note>original: Sand; a container of sand heated to provide even, controlled temperature to the glass vessel.</note>, apply fire from above, then from below, until the **Mercury** <note>original: ☿, the symbol for Mercurius/Quicksilver.</note> has come over [distilled]. When it is then taken out, the **Silver** (as is the common opinion) will have been transformed into a **dead calx** <term>todten Kalck</term> <note>A "calx" is the crumbly, ash-like substance left after a metal has been burned or oxidized.</note>. On the other hand, the pieces of Cinnabar lie entirely blackish in the glass; these, when they are melted, will reveal themselves to be a singular kind of **Silver**. Now let someone tell me, says the author of this experiment, how this **Pythagorean Transmutation** <note>A term used by some alchemists to describe a transformation governed by harmonic or numerical proportions, referencing the philosopher Pythagoras.</note> of the Silver occurs? The visual evidence is there, to which I have directed the reader. This experiment is correct and succeeds every time; although there is as yet no profit to be made in this manner, the truth [of the principle] is nonetheless present. Now, however, I will communicate several others, which [produce] such a rich...

## Chapter 15.

### Another similar Cinnabar experiment, through which the Cinnabar is transformed into Gold or Silver.

If one wishes to derive a profit from **Quicksilver** <note>original: ☿ (Mercurius).</note>, one must plant the **soul** <note>original: Seele / Anima. In alchemy, the "soul" refers to the essential quality or color-giving principle of a metal.</note> of a perfect metal into it, as can be seen from the experiment above, which we use as the foundation of our **Cinnabar** <term>Zinober</term> <note>Cinnabar is a bright red mineral, mercury sulfide, which alchemists believed could be reverted to mercury or transformed into higher metals.</note> work; there, the **soul** <note>original: Anima.</note> of the **Silver** <note>original: ☽ (Luna).</note> was drawn into the **Quicksilver** <note>original: ☿.</note> or into the pieces of Cinnabar, while the **Silver** remained behind, dead and deanimated. This experiment is also established upon such a foundation.

Take the whites of several eggs, well-beaten together, and moisten the **Cinnabar**, which must be in small pieces, with them. Afterwards, if you wish to transform the Cinnabar into **Gold** <note>original: ☉ (Sol).</note>, roll the pieces over filed **Gold**; but if you wish to transform it into **Silver** <note>original: ☽.</note>, take fine filed **Silver**, which has also been **purified** <note>original: purgiret.</note> of its **Venereal soul** <note>original: Venerischen Anima. "Venereal" refers to Venus, the planetary name for copper; the author is suggesting the silver must be cleansed of copper impurities.</note> by means of **Antimony** <note>original: 🜔 (Antimonium).</note> and **Sal ammoniac** <note>original: 🜹 (Salmiak).</note>, and roll the pieces over it, so that the **Gold** or **Silver** filings cling to them everywhere. Then, take a good, sturdy **crucible** <note>original: Tiegel.</note> that can withstand the fire; place at the bottom a layer of **Iron filings** <note>original: ♂-Feile (Mars filings).</note>, as thick as a **Reichsthaler** <note>A large silver coin used as a measurement for thickness.</note>. Upon this, make a **layer** <term>Stratum</term> <note>A layer of material in a vessel, often used in "stratification" where different substances are laid one on top of the other.</note> of filed **Gold** or **Silver**—depending on which metal you wish to obtain from the Cinnabar—about half a thaler's thickness; then a layer of the coated Cinnabar pieces

[...placed] so that only one piece of cinnabar lies next to another, and no thicker; upon this, another layer <term>stratum</term> <note>Latin for layer.</note> of filed gold <note>original: ☉ (Sol)</note> or silver <note>original: ☽ (Luna)</note>, and finally another layer of iron <note>original: ♂ (Mars)</note> filings; this is done repeatedly until the crucible <term>Tiegel</term> is full. For 1 pound of cinnabar pieces, at least 2 pounds of fine metal—either gold or silver, whichever you choose—must be used.

Once the layering <term>stratification</term> is complete, another crucible is placed on top as a cover and well-sealed with a good lute <term>Luto</term> <note>Lute or 'Lutum' was a paste used by alchemists to seal joints and vessels to make them airtight and heat-resistant.</note> that holds in the fire <note>original: △ (Ignis)</note> and does not let the spirits <note>Volatile vapors.</note> escape. Once the lute is thoroughly dry, place the crucible in sand and bury it completely. This sand must be in an iron pot and placed upon a distillation furnace. Then, give it gentle fire from above for 24 consecutive hours; after that, place fire in the furnace and increase the heat by degrees <term>per Gradus</term> <note>A technique of slowly increasing the temperature over a set period of time.</note> both below and above for 48 hours. 

After this time has passed, let the fire extinguish on its own and remove the cinnabar pieces. To the eye, they will all appear to have been transformed into lead <note>original: ♄ (Saturn)</note>; they will also cut like lead and melt as easily as any other lead, yet upon testing, they will yield a fixed metal <note>A 'fixed' metal is one that remains stable and does not evaporate or degrade when subjected to intense heat.</note>. The silver or gold, however, will lie there dead and deanimated <term>disanimiret</term> <note>Meaning the 'soul' or essential metallic principle has been extracted from the metal, leaving it a 'dead' body.</note>, and will also be somewhat lighter in weight; however, it can be restored to its previous state, as will be described hereafter. Thus, one recovers the silver or gold without any loss, and has as profit the entire amount of cinnabar, which [has been turned] into silver or...

<summary>This page details the technical process of layering cinnabar with gold or silver filings and heating them in a sealed crucible to transfer the metal's essence. The result is a substance that looks like lead but contains precious metal, while the donor metal is left "deanimated" but recoverable.</summary>

## Chapter XVI.
### Another beautiful and profitable Cinnabar-Work.

First, an **Oil of Mercury** <term>Oleum Mercurii</term> is necessary for this, through which the fixed cinnabar is made liquid, which is as follows:

The joints must be well-sealed with lute <term>lutiret</term>; thus you will obtain a blood-red oil that dissolves <term>solviret</term> all bodies.

## Chapter XVI.
### Another beautiful and profitable cinnabar-work.

This should be cleanly collected, slightly **dephlegmated** <term>dephlegmiret</term> <note>Distilled to remove the watery, inactive portion (the phlegm) and concentrate the substance.</note>, and then fitted into a **receiver** <note>The vessel in a distillation apparatus that collects the condensed vapors.</note>. The joints must be well **luted** <term>lutiret</term> <note>Sealed with an airtight alchemical paste.</note>; you will then obtain a blood-red oil which **dissolves** <term>solviret</term> all metallic bodies.

### Chapter XVII.
### A Powder of Projection for Purified Silver <term>Lunam</term>.

There is a certain kind of **Antimony Ore** <term>Minera Antimonii</term> that is of an entirely golden nature and is obtained in Hungary; it has beautiful yellow and blue stripes or rays, and otherwise all sorts of beautiful colors. This substance possesses uncommon powers within it for both humans and metals. After the true, undetermined **Universal Matter** <note>Also known as the *Prima Materia*, the foundational substance from which all things are created.</note> of the Mineral Kingdom, it is the most excellent of all. **Basilius** <note>Basilius Valentinus, a legendary 15th-century alchemist and author of influential texts on antimony.</note> wrote an entire book about it titled the ***Triumphal Chariot*** <note>original: *Triumph-Wagen* (refers to the *Currus Triumphalis Antimonii*).</note> and taught how to make the **Stone of Fire** <term>Stein Ignis</term> from it; this also succeeds quite well according to his description with this ore, though it must not be the common, ordinary kind. We wish now to communicate to the eager seeker of the **Art** <note>The "Art" refers to Alchemy.</note> a brief experiment using this **subject** <note>The material being worked upon.</note> which yields a rich benefit; it is not only effective upon metals but is also, after the **Philosopher's Stone** <term>Lapide</term> <note>original: *Lapide*, shorthand for *Lapis Philosophorum*.</note>, the highest medicine for the human body.

Take common **mercury** <term>☿</term>, dissolve it in **strong water** <term>Aq. fort.</term> <note>original: *Aqua fortis*; nitric acid.</note>, and precipitate it into a **lime** <note>original: *Kalck*; a chemical powder or oxide resulting from precipitation.</note>.

# Chapter XVIII.

## Another excellent Projection Powder / 1 part to 10 parts Mercury or Silver.

This work we have already incorporated into our recently published **Theoretical-Practical Theo-Philosophy** <note>original: "Theo-Philosophiæ Theoretico-Practicæ"</note>; yet with such obscure words that no one, unless made worthy by GOD, will easily understand it. But now, we wish to repeat it once more from our own **manual labor** <term>Hand-Arbeit</term> with the true practical instructions, out of pure love for our poor neighbor; and the process is as follows:

After one has composed themselves into a good **diet** <term>Diæt</term> in eating and drinking, and has chosen a pure wine for their beverage, then one keeps the two superfluous materials <note>In certain alchemical traditions, "superfluous materials" refers to substances like urine or sweat produced by the body after consuming specific items, believed to contain a refined "universal spirit."</note>, puts the moist and the dry together, and **distills** <term>destillirt</term> all watery moisture from it until dry, after having first closed the materials well and let them stand and **ferment** <term>fermentiren</term> for 40 days. But beforehand, before the **receiver** <term>Recipient</term> is attached, there appear white, thick, and stinking **dregs** <term>fæces</term> <note>original: "fæces"; in alchemy, this refers to the impure waste or "sediment" that is discarded before the valuable essence arrives.</note>, which one first allows to pass away, because they serve nothing for the Work. Seal this oil well and keep it for further use.

# A LITTLE BUNDLE,
## *of Recondite Alchemical Truths,*
### Alchemical.

## Chapter I.
### A Tincture by which the long life of man in this world is preserved / which also at the same time makes all metals perfect.

Take the sulfur <term>Sulphur</term> from the mountains, wherein the gold <term>☉</term> grows; grind this fine, and refine it into a concentrate <term>Schlich</term> <note>original: "Schlich"; refers to finely ground and washed ore or mineral sludge ready for further processing.</note>, so that it becomes quite pure; dry it as well as possible, place it in a cucurbit <term>Cucurbita</term> <note>A gourd-shaped glass vessel used as the lower part of a distillation apparatus.</note>, pour upon it the following water so that it covers it considerably, seal the glass with diligence, and set it to putrefy <term>putrificiren</term> in horse manure <note>original: "Roß-Mist"; used in alchemy as a source of gentle, consistent heat from natural fermentation.</note> for one month. After that, distill <term>destillir</term> the water off until it reaches an oily consistency; pour fresh water upon it, let it stand in a water bath <term>B. M.</term> <note>original: "Balneum Mariae" or "Mary's Bath," a vessel of warm water used to heat laboratory equipment gently.</note> for 8 days and nights. After that, draw off the wateriness until the oily consistency is reached; then set the glass with the matter in a coal fire <term>△</term> <note>The triangle symbol represents Fire.</note> from the side <term>per latus</term>, into a receiver <note>original: "Fürleg"; the vessel used to catch the distillate.</note>. Distill it gently at first, then more strongly, and a blood-red oil will rise over. Maintain the fire with steady heat until everything has come over, for

## Chapter III.

### The Red Lion / a Tincture, which transforms all metals into the highest metal <note>In the alchemical hierarchy, the "highest metal" is Gold.</note>.

Many great secrets appear in all things of Nature / which the highest GOD Himself has ordained / so that even the children of men <note>An archaic term for humanity or the human race.</note> marvel at them / when such things are revealed to them. The great treasures lie in the mountains of the earth / from which humans must nourish themselves and sustain their lives in this world. For without the soil, man cannot live in this world / from which food and drink come / and we humans are not worthy of these gifts / which the merciful GOD sends to us; everything is misused / with ingratitude toward the almighty GOD / for which reason He does not allow many treasures to be revealed / and thus many arts remain hidden in this world because of our ignorance / yet they are revealed more to pious people / than they might expect / as has often been seen / that a poor man has many times struck upon something great of Gold <term>☉</term> and Silver <term>☽</term> <note>original: "☉ und ☽"; these are the standard alchemical symbols for the Sun (Gold) and Moon (Silver).</note>.

Now the Red Lion <term>rothe Löwe</term> <note>The "Red Lion" is a celebrated symbol for the completed Philosopher’s Stone in its most potent, "red" state, capable of transmuting metals and acting as a universal medicine.</note> is the greatest treasure in this world / with which the children of men / for whom it is intended / can perform great wonders / so that they may attain health and wealth.

### Chapter V.

## On the Secrets of <term>Vitriol</term> <note>original: the symbol ⊖. Vitriol (iron or copper sulfate) was one of the most important substances in alchemy, valued for its ability to produce powerful acids.</note>, and how it is to be brought into a Redness. <note>original: "in eine R." --- Page 128 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of Chapter V regarding Vitriol. It elaborates on the allegorical and physical nature of the substance, specifically interpreting the famous alchemical acronym "V.I.T.R.I.O.L."</meta>

...it has been the custom that they recorded and made known their **secrets** <note>original: "Secreta"</note> either in obscured riddles, histories, fables, or images; or else composed them in hidden philosophical sayings, maxims, and rhymes. This they have also done with **vitriol** <note>original: "Vitriol." In alchemy, this usually refers to iron or copper sulfate, prized for the acidic "spirit" that could be distilled from it.</note>, saying: "Visit the interior of the earth; by rectifying, you will find the hidden stone, the true medicine." <note>original: "Visitetis interiora Terræ, rectificando, invenietis occultum Lapidem veram medicinam." This is the famous alchemical motto, where the first letters of each word spell out V-I-T-R-I-O-L.</note> To understand this correctly, the words themselves give the proper sense, which we explain or interpret thus: Since the ancients say that one should visit the earth by rectifying <note>To "rectify" means to purify a substance through repeated distillation or refinement.</note>, this "rectifying" is not to be understood as referring to common earth, but to the mountains of vitriol.

Furthermore, they say that the inner parts of the earth should be visited, and not the outer. Thus, vitriol is outwardly green in color, as can be plainly seen, but inwardly the **philosophers** <note>original: "Philosophi." In this context, it refers specifically to alchemical adepts.</note> find it to be red. To the tongue, it tastes sharp and bitter on the outside, yet inwardly it is lovely and sweet. To the hands, it feels hard and coarse on the outside, yet inwardly it is delicate and lovely, being such a subtle juice, **balsam** <note>A fragrant, oily, and healing resinous substance.</note>, and pure oil. To the nose, its scent feels quite wild and crude, like **sulfur** <note>original: "Schwefel."</note>, yet in its interior it is more noble and lovely than **ambergris** <note>original: "Ambra," a rare and highly valued aromatic substance found in the ocean.</note> or a balsam, and is perceived as such.

Although many philosophers have dreamed of this, or learned of it from other signs of nature...

...would be; thus they would bring themselves to a blood-red oil and juice, and **transmute** <note>original: "transmutiren." ### Note.
### How one should recognize the Stone, whether it be of the right material.

Firstly, small stones are found, coarse-textured like chestnuts; some pieces are pointed, and within them are mixed small stones streaked with veins like sapphire, emerald, or garnet, and many other colors. Often, **marcasite** <term>Marcafit</term> <note>a brittle metallic mineral, often iron disulfide, frequently used as a source for sulfur or vitriol in alchemy</note> is found, both coarse and fine, in the aforementioned pieces; and if a portion of it is dissolved in common water and allowed to form a residue, one finds within it several little gold flakes, small pieces of marcasite, and granulated **quicksilver** <note>original: Argentum vivum</note>, as well as small colored stones, as was mentioned. If these signs are present in the **Stone** <note>original: Lapide</note> or ore, you have a true sign that it is the correct ore; otherwise, do not work [with it].

Also note: before the **matter** is put into the glass, it should be purged for 7 days in a glass vessel according to the **philosophical method** <note>original: Philosophischer Meynung; referring to the established traditions of the alchemical masters</note>, for the matter is granular, consisting of many—

## Chapter VII.
### Tincture with Venus, Mars, and Mercury.
<note>In alchemy, these names refer to the metals Copper (Venus), Iron (Mars), and Quicksilver (Mercury)</note>

Take 8 **Loth** <note>a German unit of weight, roughly 1/2 ounce or 15 grams</note> of the best **Hüttenberg steel** <note>steel from the Hüttenberg region in Austria, famous since antiquity for high-quality iron ore</note>; **dissolve** <term>solvir</term> it in **oil of mercury** <term>☿ Oli</term>, and when it will dissolve no more, then pour a common water into it; and when it has all dissolved, then filter it through paper and let it evaporate into an oil. Set it in a cold place, so that beautiful **crystals** form, which are very sweet; keep these, but let the water evaporate into an oil again; do this until no more crystals form. Collect them all together, 8 Loth, and keep it.

Secondly, take a half pound <term>℔ß.</term> <note>original: ℔ß; the symbol ℔ denotes a pound, and ß is a shorthand for "semis," meaning half</note> of **antimony** <term>♁</term> and a half pound of **mercury** <term>☿</term>; rub these 2 pieces together as finely as possible and distill the oil from them through the **retort** <term>Retorten</term>. At the last, give it a strong fire, so it **sublimates** <note>the process where a solid turns into vapor and then back to solid without becoming liquid</note> in the retort into **cinnabar** <term>Zinober</term> <note>mercury sulfide, a bright red mineral</note>. Keep that separately, and at the bottom a powder remains; rub that small again, and take as much again,

### Composition.

### No. III.

### To bring 5 **Loth** <note>A unit of weight; 16 Loth typically made up one "Mark."</note> of Gold <term>☉</term> into the Mark of Silver <term>☽</term>.

Arsenicum album <note>White arsenic.</note>, common Sulfur, and Antimony, two ounces <term>℥</term> <note>original: "ana ℥ ij," meaning "of each 2 ounces."</note> of each. Take the other half part; and an equal weight of fine Tin <term>♃</term>; and calcine it with Sulfur <term>🜍</term> in a clean pot between two bricks in a coal fire until the tin lies there like an ore <note>original: "Gruben." This describes the crumbly, unrefined appearance of the metal after calcination.</note>. Mix that with half as much Cinnabar <note>Mercury sulfide.</note>; let the cinnabar smoke away from it. Take the tin, and an equal weight of the above-mentioned precipitate; into this knead <note>original: "impastir."</note> raw Mercury <term>☿</term> of equal weight. Let them melt strongly together, and add four **Loth** of Lead <term>♄</term>. Thus a regulus <note>original: "König" (king). The purified metallic mass that settles at the bottom of a crucible.</note> will settle. Drive this <note>Refine the metal using high heat.</note> with an equal weight of fine Silver <term>☽</term> (which has also been previously cemented and purged by grinding with Vitriol <term>🜔</term> and Salmiac <term>🜹</term> <note>Ammonium chloride.</note>) on the cupel <note>original: "Scherben." A shallow clay dish used in assaying.</note> and afterwards to the brightening <note>original: "Blicken." The sudden flash of light seen when the lead has been fully oxidized and the silver becomes pure.</note>; the Silver <term>☽</term> [then] contains 5 **Loth** of Gold <term>☉</term> in the mark.

Take further 24 **Loth** <note>A unit of weight; 16 Loth typically made up one "Mark," roughly 1/2 pound.</note> of white **Copper** <term>♀</term>, good English **Tin** <term>♃</term>, and **Antimony** <term>♁</term>, one **pound** <term>℔</term> <note>original: "j."</note> of each, and 4 **Loth** of **Vitriol** <term>⊕</term>. Let these melt together strongly, and pour out the **regulus** <term>King</term> <note>original: "König." The "king" or regulus is the heavy, purified metal that settles at the bottom of a crucible, separated from the slag.</note>; keep it. Let the **slag** <note>The stony waste matter separated from metals during smelting.</note> melt again, and pour another **regulus**, and add it to the previous ones; do this with the slag until no more **regulus** settles.

Let all these **reguli** smoke away well until an **ore** <note>original: "Gruben." This refers to the crumbly, calcined residue or "calx" left after the more volatile parts have evaporated.</note> remains; grind this small and keep it for necessity. Take the **slag**, ground small, lay it on a **dissolving stone** <note>original: "Solvir-Stein." A stone used for deliquescence, where a substance absorbs moisture from the air until it dissolves.</note> and moisten it with **Water** <term>▽</term>; thus, in a cold place, a water will run down within several days. Now take the four **sulfurs** <note>likely referring to the sulfurous components prepared in the preceding steps</note> mentioned above, ground small together, and among them the **ore** from the **regulus**; put everything into a glass, and pour all of the **common water** <note>likely the water collected from the dissolving stone</note> over it. Let it gently **coagulate** <note>to thicken or solidify by evaporation</note> to dryness in a sand bath, then it will begin to flow; let it go for a while, then let it cool. Afterwards, grind it small and keep it in an earthen vessel, provided that it stands constantly in the warmth. Guard yourself well against the smoke in the fire and while grinding, using fresh butter and **wormwood wine** <note>Wormwood and fats like butter were historically used as folk remedies or "antidotes" to protect against the toxic fumes of heavy metals and arsenic.</note>.

Take 2 **Loth** of the **cement-powder**; 1 **Loth** of fine granulated **Silver** <term>☽</term> that has previously been **purified** through **Saltpeter** <term>①</term> and **Sal Ammoniac** <term>🜹</term>. Put them together into a **crucible** <term>Tiegel</term>; let it heat for 9 hours: three hours gently with a mild **Fire** <term>△</term>, and so on strongly and even stronger. Afterwards, carry the matter into **Lead** <term>♄</term> and drive it to the **flash** <note>original: "Blicken." The sudden brightening or "flash" of a molten bead of silver or gold during cupellation, signaling that all base metals have been oxidized and removed.</note>; that is a magnificent Gold in all tests.

->No. 5.<-
### An Introduction into Silver: 4 Loth of Gold into the Mark.

Take: **Antimony** <term>♁</term> and **Arsenic**, 4 **Loth** <note>A unit of weight; 16 Loth typically made up one "Mark," roughly 1/2 pound.</note> of each, ground fine. Take thin sheets of **Copper** <term>♀</term> and arrange them layer upon layer <note>original: "S.S.S." for *stratum super stratum*</note> in a crucible. **Calcine** <note>To heat a substance to high temperatures, often to oxidize it or reduce it to a powder.</note> this, and then, having ground it small, pour **Aqua Fortis** <note>Nitric acid, a powerful solvent used to separate metals.</note> over it. Let it extract the essence of the **Antimony** and **Copper**. Afterward, take finely granulated **Silver** <term>☽</term>—which has been previously **cemented** <note>Refined through a dry heating process using reactive salts.</note> and purified five times with **Salt** <term>🜔</term> and **Sal Ammoniac** <term>🜹</term>—and place it in a glass; use 4 **Loth** of each. Pour the **Aqua Fortis** over it and **precipitate** it. Melt this with an equal weight of the following **flux**. Refine the **regulus** <note>original: "König" (King). The heavy, purified metal that settles at the bottom of a crucible.</note>, and the **Mark** will contain 4 **Loth** of **Gold** <term>☉</term>.

**Note:** If you mix the mass of **Silver**—after the **Aqua Fortis** has been poured over it and it has been precipitated, but before it is **reduced** <note>Returned to its metallic form from a salt or oxide.</note>—with an equal weight of pure **Mercury** <term>☿</term>, and melt them together so that the **Mercury** smokes away cleanly, then pulverize it and burn it off with wax before finally reducing it, you will achieve even better results.

### Flux.

One **Loth** of **Litharge** <note>Lead oxide, often called "silver-glance" or "Silber-Glätte" in German.</note>, 4 **Loth** of common **Salt** <term>🜔</term>, 2 **Loth** of raw **Copper** <term>♀</term>, and half a **Loth** of **Iron filings**. I tell you, this flux is beyond price because of its quality; it creates much benefit in **antimonial** <note>original: "♁ ialischen"</note> matters.

### The Oil.

Take eight **Loth** or more of **Lead Litharge** <term>♄ Glette</term> <note>Lead oxide, often used as a starting material in alchemical recipes.</note>; grind it small and fine. Once placed in a glass, pour a strong **Vinegar** <term>🜊</term> upon it and let it extract for several hours. Draw off this vinegar, and a powder will remain. Grind this again, place it in a glass, and pour a strong **Brandy** <note>original: "Brandtewein." Usually a distilled spirit or alcohol used as a solvent.</note> over it which contains no water. Let it extract well for several hours. This is then drawn off until it reaches an oily consistency; thus it is ready to be used with **Mercury** <term>☿</term>, and also to make a **fixed Sulfur** <term>🜍</term> with it.

### No. 9.

### How to ignite the **Silver's** <term>☽</term> **fire** <term>🜂</term> and bring it to the weight of **Gold** <term>☉</term>.

Take **Quicksilver** <term>☿</term> and **Sal Ammoniac** <term>🜹</term>, 4 Loth of **each** <note>original: "ana," a pharmaceutical term meaning equal parts of each ingredient.</note>. Rub both well together. Then take 4 Loth of finely filed **Copper** <term>♀</term> and make a layer with the **Quicksilver** <term>☿</term> mixture at the bottom of the vessel, and a layer of the **Copper** <term>♀</term> on top of it. Continue this **layer upon layer** until you have no more left. Place this, well-secured and plugged at the top, into a moderate charcoal fire until it flows and has formed into a lump. Once that has happened, let it cool; place it in a very cold location, and within a day and night it will turn yellow-green. Keep this until you have need of it.

Afterward, take one and a half pounds of **grey Sulfur**, 8 Loth <note>A historic unit of weight, roughly 15 grams or half an ounce.</note> of **Verdigris**, and one and a half pounds of **Quicklime** that is clean. In this way, a **treasure-rich oil** will come over; keep this with great diligence.

Do this for the seventh time, and a magnificent **Precipitate** will come of it. Test it to see if it smokes away; if it does not smoke and it flows, then it is **fixed** <note>original: "fix." In alchemy, a substance is "fixed" when it can withstand the heat of a fire without evaporating.</note>. If not, you must coagulate more oil into it.

### No 10.
### How one can noble tin into **silver** <term>silver</term>, so that it also contains some **gold** <term>gold</term>.

One takes English **tin**, which is turned into ashes with **lead** and common **salt** <term>salt</term> <note>original: "🜔", the symbol for common salt.</note>. One boils these ashes in common water so that the salt is removed, then dries them with moderate heat. Mix this with **antimony** <term>antimony</term> <note>original: "⦶"</note> that has been **fixed** by coals and made very "penetrating" through multiple **stratification** and **cementation** <note>A process of heating a metal with powdered reagents in layers to alter its properties.</note> with **quicklime** <note>original: "Calce viva."</note>. Put this into a well-**luted** pot <note>A pot sealed with "Lutum," a specialized alchemical clay or paste, to make it airtight.</note> so that the air can in no way reach it, nor the spirits fly away, which would otherwise ruin the entire work. When the **lute** <note>The clay sealant.</note> is well dried, place the pot in a **calcination fire** <note>A high-heat fire used to oxidize or chemically change a substance.</note> in a glass-furnace for about 24 hours. Afterward, take it from the fire and let it cool on its own. Pulverize the matter found within and separate it from the **fixed nitre** <note>original: "Nitro fixo," likely referring to potassium carbonate or a similar salt residue remaining after the high-heat process.</note>.

However, because it decreases in **quantity** <note>original Latin: "quantitate".</note> through much melting, one must add new material when one sees this happening.
